I mean, I understand why you would ask that, but I'm not misinterpreting anything. \n\nFor the past year I’ve been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad’s lab results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine and that I must be mistaken, I know the chart doesn’t match what I saw. I just know it. \n\nIt’s... it's really wearing me down, having to constantly defend what I know is true when nobody else will look at the facts. But no, I don't have any doubts about what I'm seeing.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your dad's lab results in the chart and compare them to what you saw or remember, can you walk me through one specific example—like, what was the test name, what did you observe about the blood sample, and what numbers appeared in the chart that didn't match?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really have a specific example like that. It's not about the color of the blood or anything I observed when they drew it. I'm a phlebotomist, so I know how the blood looks doesn't tell you the numbers. It's just... when I look at the chart, the numbers aren't right. I don't have one specific test name or exact number to give you right now, but I know what I'm looking at, and I know they've been altered. It's the whole picture of his results that just doesn't add up to what it should be.\n\nDoctor: You've been focused on your dad's lab results for a year now. In that time, have you developed any other beliefs about what might be going wrong with his medical care—like problems with his medications, his diagnoses, or how the doctors are treating him—or is it really just the lab results that concern you?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the lab results. I haven't noticed anything weird with his medications or... or how the doctors are treating him in general. His diagnoses make sense to me. \n\nIt's literally just the numbers in the system. I'm not trying to find problems with his whole care plan or anything like that. The doctors are doing their jobs fine with the treatment part. I just want the chart to actually reflect his real lab work. That's the only thing I'm concerned about, because that's the only thing that's being altered.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that when you look at your dad's blood sample and then look at the chart numbers, they don't match up. But I want to understand this better—are you saying that you actually have access to see his original lab results or blood samples side-by-side with what's in the chart, or are you comparing what you remember from seeing the blood to what you see in the chart later?\n\nPatient: I think you misunderstood what I said earlier. I'm a phlebotomist, so I know you can't just look at a vial of blood and know what the numbers are going to be. That's not what I'm doing. \n\nI don't have the original lab reports sitting in front of me to compare side-by-side with the chart, either. If I did, I wouldn't be having this problem, because I could just show everyone the proof. \n\nIt's just that when I look at his chart in the system, I know the numbers have been changed. I know what his results should look like, and the ones in the system are wrong. They're just... they're not the real results. I don't know how else to explain it, but I'm not comparing a tube of blood to a computer screen. I just know the chart is altered.\n\nDoctor: Besides the belief about your dad's lab results being changed, do you have any other strong beliefs about things that are happening—for example, beliefs that people are trying to harm you, that you're being monitored or controlled, or that there are hidden meanings in everyday events—or is the lab result issue really the only thing you're preoccupied with?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any beliefs like that. Nobody is monitoring me or trying to harm me, and there aren't any hidden meanings in things. It really is just the lab results. \n\nI'm basically just here because for the past year, I've been trying to prove that someone in the clinic system is changing or holding back parts of my dad's lab results. And no matter how many doctors or family members tell me the numbers are fine, I know the chart doesn't match what I saw. It's just... it's really wearing me down, honestly, trying to get people to see it. But no, nothing else like what you're asking about. Just the labs."
  }
